# OSDC — 2026-09-03

> 36 advisories analyzed

## GHSA-fph3-ghq9-vw66 · github.com/siyuan-note/siyuan/kernel

2026-09-03 · Go · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→SQL`

### Root cause

The application directly concatenated user-supplied input into SQL queries and regular expressions without proper sanitization or parameterization. Specifically, the `fullTextSearchAssetContent` function, when `method` was set to 2 (SQL) or 3 (Regexp), allowed unauthenticated users to inject arbitrary SQL or regular expression syntax.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could execute arbitrary SQL commands on the underlying database, leading to data exfiltration, modification, or deletion. Additionally, they could perform REGEXP injection, potentially causing denial of service or information disclosure through crafted regular expressions.

### Key diff

```
--- a/kernel/model/asset_content.go
+++ b/kernel/model/asset_content.go
@@ -74,10 +75,12 @@ func GetAssetContent(id, query string, queryMethod int) (ret *AssetContent) {
 
 	table := "asset_contents_fts_case_insensitive"
-	filter := " id = '" + id + "'"
+	filter := "id = ?"
+	args := []any{id}
 	if "" != query {
-		filter += " AND `" + table + "` MATCH '" + buildAssetContentColumnFilter() + ":(" + query + ")'"
+		filter += " AND `" + table + "` MATCH ?"
+		args = append(args, buildAssetContentColumnFilter()+":("+query+")")
 	}
 
 	projections := "id, name, ext, path, size, updated, " +
-		highlight(" + table + ", 6, '" + search.SearchMarkLeft + "', '" + search.SearchMarkRight + "') AS content"
-	stmt := "SELECT " + projections + " FROM " + table + " WHERE " + filter
-	assetContents := sql.SelectAssetContentsRawStmt(stmt, 1, 1)
+		highlight(" + table + ", 6, '" + search.SearchMarkLeft + "', '" + search.SearchMarkRight + "') AS content"
+	stmt := "SELECT " + projections + " FROM " + table + " WHERE " + filter
+	assetContents := sql.SelectAssetContentsRawStmtNoParseArgs(stmt, args, 1)
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors SQL query construction to use parameterized queries (prepared statements) via `?` placeholders and `sql.SelectAssetContentsRawStmtNoParseArgs` for all affected functions. It also modifies the regular expression search to use parameterized queries for the `REGEXP` operator, preventing direct concatenation of user input into the regex pattern.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 31 occurrences**

## GHSA-q2vg-7qgx-x5fc · github.com/siyuan-note/siyuan/kernel

2026-09-03 · Go · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→SQL`

### Root cause

The application constructed SQL queries by directly concatenating user-controlled input (mentionKeywords and keyword) into the FTS MATCH clause without proper escaping. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary SQL into the query by crafting malicious input containing double quotes, breaking out of the intended string literal.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary SQL commands within the database, potentially leading to data exfiltration, modification, or deletion, and could bypass intended access controls.

### Key diff

```
- buf.WriteString("\"" + mentionKeyword + "\"")
+ buf.WriteString(quoteFTSPhrase(mentionKeyword))
...
- sqlBlocks := sql.SelectBlocksRawStmtInBox(query, 1, Conf.Search.Limit, boxID)
+ sqlBlocks := sql.SelectBlocksRawStmtArgsInBox(query, args, Conf.Search.Limit, boxID)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `quoteFTSPhrase` function to properly escape double quotes in user input for FTS queries. It also refactors the query construction to use parameterized queries via `sql.SelectBlocksRawStmtArgsInBox`, ensuring that user input is treated as data rather than executable code.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 31 occurrences**

## GHSA-99rq-75j6-5j9f · github.com/siyuan-note/siyuan/kernel

2026-09-03 · Go · **HIGH** 8.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The previous SVG sanitization logic used an HTML parser (golang.org/x/net/html) to process SVG content. This approach was vulnerable because HTML and XML (SVG's native format) have different parsing rules, allowing attackers to craft malicious SVG payloads that would bypass the HTML-based sanitizer but be interpreted as active content (e.g., JavaScript) when rendered by an SVG-aware client.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious JavaScript into SVG content, leading to stored or reflected Cross-Site Scripting (XSS). This could allow them to steal user cookies, session tokens, deface web pages, or redirect users to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
--- kernel/util/misc.go
+++ kernel/util/misc.go
-func SanitizeSVG(svgInput string) string {
-	// 1. 将字符串解析为节点树
-	doc, err := html.Parse(strings.NewReader(svgInput))
+func SanitizeSVG(svgInput string) (string, error) {
+	decoder := xml.NewDecoder(strings.NewReader(svgInput))
+	decoder.Strict = true
```

### Fix summary

The patch completely rewrites the SVG sanitization function to use an XML parser (encoding/xml) with strict parsing rules. It now explicitly walks the XML token stream, enforcing limits on depth and token count, and specifically filters out unsafe elements (like <script>) and attributes (like 'on*' event handlers or 'javascript:' URIs) based on XML semantics, preventing parser-differential attacks.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 107 occurrences**
> Languages: Python, JavaScript, PHP, Java, Go, RUST, Ruby

## GHSA-69mh-gvh4-8gp7 · github.com/siyuan-note/siyuan/kernel

2026-09-03 · Go · **HIGH** 8.6 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The application's API endpoints for retrieving heading-related transactions (`getHeadingLevelTransaction`, `getHeadingDeleteTransaction`, `getHeadingInsertTransaction`) lacked proper authorization checks. While `model.CheckAuth` ensured a user was logged in, it did not verify if the user had the necessary administrative or publishing privileges to access the content of documents that were marked as publish-disabled.

### Impact

An authenticated but unauthorized user (e.g., a regular reader) could access the full content of documents that were intended to be private or publish-disabled, leading to unauthorized information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
-	ginServer.Handle("POST", "/api/block/getHeadingLevelTransaction", model.CheckAuth, getHeadingLevelTransaction)
+	ginServer.Handle("POST", "/api/block/getHeadingLevelTransaction", model.CheckAuth, model.CheckAdminRole, getHeadingLevelTransaction)
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds `model.CheckAdminRole` to the affected API endpoints (`/api/block/getHeadingLevelTransaction`, `/api/block/getHeadingDeleteTransaction`, `/api/block/getHeadingInsertTransaction`). This ensures that only users with administrative privileges can access these endpoints, thereby preventing unauthorized access to sensitive document content.

## GHSA-7j72-f6wg-cxw6 · github.com/siyuan-note/siyuan/kernel

2026-09-03 · Go · **HIGH** 8.6 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

Several API endpoints (getHeadingChildrenDOM, getHeading*Transaction, getBacklinkDoc) that should have enforced publish-password authentication in publish mode were missing the necessary access control checks. This allowed unauthenticated users to access content that should have been protected.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could bypass the publish-password protection and access the content of published documents, potentially including sensitive information, even when the document was configured to require a password.

### Key diff

```
--- a/kernel/api/block.go
+++ b/kernel/api/block.go
@@ -216,6 +220,10 @@ func getHeadingChildrenDOM(c *gin.Context) {
 	}
 
 	id := arg["id"].(string)
+	if !checkBlockPublishAccess(c, id, ret) {
+		return
+	}
 
 	removeFoldAttr := true
 	if nil != arg["removeFoldAttr"] {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces calls to `checkBlockPublishAccess` in the affected API endpoints (`getHeadingChildrenIDs`, `getHeadingChildrenDOM`, `getDocInfo`, `getBlockInfo`). It also renames `checkBlockInfoPublishAccess` to `checkBlockPublishAccess` and updates its callers, ensuring consistent and correct access control for published blocks.

## GHSA-mw8r-mw84-88v2 · github.com/siyuan-note/siyuan/kernel

2026-09-03 · Go · **HIGH** 8.6 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The application's WebSocket broadcast mechanism did not properly distinguish between authenticated and unauthenticated (publish service) sessions. This allowed anonymous readers connected to the publish service to receive a live, unfiltered feed of all edits, including those for protected or forbidden documents, because the broadcast functions did not filter out publish-mode sessions.

### Impact

An attacker, acting as an anonymous reader, could gain unauthorized access to sensitive information by observing real-time edits of documents that should otherwise be protected or forbidden, bypassing intended access controls.

### Key diff

```
--- kernel/util/websocket.go
+++ kernel/util/websocket.go
@@ -49,6 +49,9 @@ func BroadcastByTypeAndExcludeApp(excludeApp, typ, cmd string, code int, msg str
 
 		appSessions.Range(func(key, value any) bool {
 			session := value.(*melody.Session)
+			if isPublishSession(session) {
+				return true
+			}
 			if t, ok := session.Get("type"); ok && typ == t {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new audience identifier for publish service JWT tokens and modifies the token validation logic to explicitly check for this audience. Crucially, it updates all WebSocket broadcast functions to explicitly skip sessions identified as 'publish sessions', preventing them from receiving internal, unfiltered updates.

## GHSA-vpjw-wf5h-cgpq · github.com/siyuan-note/siyuan/kernel

2026-09-03 · Go · **HIGH** 8.6 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The graph generation endpoints (getGraph, getLocalGraph) in SiYuan did not properly enforce password protection for documents when constructing the graph view. While the `FilterGraphByPublishIgnore` function filtered out nodes based on general publish ignore settings, it failed to check for password-protected documents, allowing anonymous users to see block-level content of such documents in the graph.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could access the structural information and block-level content of documents that were intended to be protected by a password, bypassing the access control mechanism.

### Key diff

```
-	func FilterGraphByPublishIgnore(publishIgnore PublishAccess, nodes []*GraphNode, links []*GraphLink) (retNodes []*GraphNode, retLinks []*GraphLink) {
+	func FilterGraphByPublishAccess(c *gin.Context, publishAccess PublishAccess, nodes []*GraphNode, links []*GraphLink) (retNodes []*GraphNode, retLinks []*GraphLink) {
 		retNodes = []*GraphNode{}
 		retLinks = []*GraphLink{}
-		ignoreNodeIDs := []string{}
+		publishIgnore := GetInvisiblePublishAccess(publishAccess)
+		nodeByID := make(map[string]*GraphNode, len(nodes))
+		virtualNodeIDs := map[string]bool{}
+		nodeBaseSizes := make(map[string]float64, len(nodes))
 		for _, node := range nodes {
-			if CheckPathAccessableByPublishIgnore(node.Box, node.Path, publishIgnore) {
-				retNodes = append(retNodes, node)
+			if node.Box == "" && node.Path == "" {
+				nodeByID[node.ID] = node
+				virtualNodeIDs[node.ID] = true
 			} else {
-				ignoreNodeIDs = append(ignoreNodeIDs, node.ID)
+				if node.Box == "" || node.Path == "" || !CheckPathAccessableByPublishIgnore(node.Box, node.Path, publishIgnore) {
+					continue
+				}
+				passwordID, password := GetPathPasswordByPublishAccess(node.Box, node.Path, publishAccess)
+				if password != "" && !CheckPublishAuthCookie(c, passwordID, password) {
+					continue
+				}
+				nodeByID[node.ID] = node
 			}
+
+			baseSize := node.Size
+			if 0 < node.Defs {
+				baseSize /= math.Log2(float64(node.Defs)) + 1
+			}
+			nodeBaseSizes[node.ID] = baseSize
 		}
-		for _, link := range links {
-			ignore := false
-			for _, ignoreNodeID := range ignoreNodeIDs {
-				if ignoreNodeID == link.From || ignoreNodeID == link.To {
-					ignore = true
-					break
-				}
-			}
-			if !ignore {
-				retLinks = append(retLinks, link)
-			}
+		filteredLinks := make([]*GraphLink, 0, len(links))
+		for _, link := range links {
+			if link.From == link.To || nodeByID[link.From] == nil || nodeByID[link.To] == nil {
+				continue
+			}
+			filteredLinks = append(filteredLinks, link)
 		}
 		return
```

### Fix summary

The patch renames `FilterGraphByPublishIgnore` to `FilterGraphByPublishAccess` and modifies its logic to explicitly check for password-protected documents using `GetPathPasswordByPublishAccess` and `CheckPublishAuthCookie`. It now correctly filters out nodes and links associated with password-protected documents if the user is not authenticated for them.

## GHSA-78x9-fhhx-v2g6 · @aborruso/ckan-mcp-server

2026-09-03 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an insecure canonicalization of cache keys. The `canonicalizeParams` function used a simple `key=value` string concatenation after sorting keys, which could lead to collisions for different input parameters (e.g., `{q:"budget",rows:10}` and `{q:"budget&rows=10"}` could produce the same cache key). Additionally, the `buildCacheKey` function did not unambiguously frame the `serverUrl`, `action`, and `params`, allowing for potential boundary confusion.

### Impact

An attacker could craft malicious requests that, due to cache key collisions, would cause the server to serve incorrect or attacker-controlled cached responses to other users. This could lead to data manipulation, denial of service, or other forms of unauthorized information exposure or alteration.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/utils/cache.ts
+++ b/src/utils/cache.ts
@@ -63,17 +63,32 @@ export function getCacheConfig(): CacheConfig {
   return { enabled, ttlDefault, maxEntries, maxEntryBytes };
 }
 
+/** Recursively sort object keys so serialization is order-independent and type-preserving. */
+function canonicalizeValue(value: unknown): unknown {
+  if (Array.isArray(value)) return value.map(canonicalizeValue);
+  if (value && typeof value === "object") {
+    const sorted: Record<string, unknown> = {};
+    for (const key of Object.keys(value as Record<string, unknown>).sort()) {
+      sorted[key] = canonicalizeValue((value as Record<string, unknown>)[key]);
+    }
+    return sorted;
+  }
+  return value;
+}
+
+/**
+ * Serialize params to an unambiguous, injection-proof canonical string. Uses typed JSON
+ * (not `k=v` joins), so `{q:"budget",rows:10}` and `{q:"budget&rows=10"}` — and a nested
+ * object vs. its stringified form — never collide onto the same cache key (GHSA-78x9).
+ */
 export function canonicalizeParams(params: Record<string, unknown>): string {
-  const keys = Object.keys(params).sort();
-  const pairs: string[] = [];
-  for (const key of keys) {
+  const filtered: Record<string, unknown> = {};
+  for (const key of Object.keys(params)) {
     const value = params[key];
     if (value === undefined || value === null) continue;
-    const serialized =
-      typeof value === "object" ? JSON.stringify(value) : String(value);
-    pairs.push(`${key}=${serialized}`);
+    filtered[key] = value;
   }
-  return pairs.join("&");
+  return JSON.stringify(canonicalizeValue(filtered));
 }
 
 async function sha1Hex(input: string): Promise<string> {
@@ -90,7 +105,8 @@ export async function buildCacheKey(
   action: string,
   params: Record<string, unknown>
 ): Promise<string> {
-  const raw = `${serverUrl}|${action}|${canonicalizeParams(params)}`;
+  // JSON-array framing so serverUrl/action/params boundaries are unambiguous too.
+  const raw = JSON.stringify([serverUrl, action, canonicalizeParams(params)]);
   return sha1Hex(raw);
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a robust canonicalization method for cache keys. It now recursively sorts object keys and uses `JSON.stringify` on the canonicalized structure to ensure order-independent and type-preserving serialization. It also frames the `serverUrl`, `action`, and canonicalized `params` within a JSON array to prevent boundary confusion. Additionally, the patch adds size limits for HTTP responses and decompressed data to mitigate potential DoS attacks.

## GHSA-6f9w-9hf2-5rg3 · @aborruso/ckan-mcp-server

2026-09-03 · JavaScript · **LOW** 3.7 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The application was reflecting verbose error messages and potentially sensitive data from external CKAN API responses or internal worker errors directly back to the caller. This could include internal hostnames, IPs, stack traces, or parts of the upstream server's response body, which might contain sensitive information.

### Impact

An attacker could gain insights into the application's internal infrastructure, network configuration, or other sensitive details by triggering errors or by redirecting the application to a malicious or non-CKAN host, turning a blind SSRF into a data exfiltration channel.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/utils/http.ts
+++ b/src/utils/http.ts
@@ -689,8 +689,19 @@ export async function makeCkanRequest<T>(
       auditLog(serverUrl, action, params, false);
       return result;
     } else {
+      // Do NOT reflect the full upstream body to the caller: if the server was
+      // pointed/redirected at a non-CKAN host it could carry internal detail,
+      // turning blind SSRF into a return-value channel (GHSA-6f9w). Log it
+      // server-side only (truncated) and return a generic, action-scoped error.
+      try {
+        if (typeof process !== "undefined") {
+          process.stderr.write(
+            `[ckan] success=false action=${action}: ${JSON.stringify(decodedData).slice(0, 500)}\n`
+          );
+        }
+      } catch { /* ignore logging failures */ }
       throw new CkanApiError(
-        `CKAN API returned success=false: ${JSON.stringify(decodedData)}`,
+        `CKAN API returned success=false for action "${action}".`,
         undefined,
         action
       );
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies error handling to prevent the reflection of detailed internal error messages or full upstream API responses to the caller. Instead, generic error messages are returned, while detailed errors are logged server-side (truncated if from an external source). Additionally, it hardens `postMessage` origin checks to prevent untrusted origins from receiving sensitive data.

## GHSA-2h9g-j24r-h63g · orval

2026-09-03 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from improper sanitization of user-controlled input when generating code. Specifically, default values for array items and route paths were directly embedded into JavaScript template literals without sufficient escaping, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary code that would be executed at import time.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution by crafting a malicious OpenAPI schema that, when processed by Orval, would generate JavaScript code containing the attacker's payload. This code would then execute when the generated module is imported.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/zod/src/index.ts
+++ b/packages/zod/src/index.ts
@@ -834,17 +852,13 @@ export const generateZodValidationSchemaDefinition = (
       defaultValue = entries.length === 0 ? `{}` : `{ ${entries} }`;
     } else {
       // OpenApiSchemaObject defines default as 'any'
-      const rawStringified = stringify(schema.default);
-      defaultValue =
-        rawStringified === undefined
-          ? 'null'
-          : rawStringified.replaceAll("'", '`');
+      defaultValue = formatDefaultValue(schema.default);
 
       // If the schema is an array with enum items, inject inplace to avoid issues with default values
       const isArrayWithEnumItems =
--- a/packages/core/src/getters/route.ts
+++ b/packages/core/src/getters/route.ts
@@ -44,13 +62,18 @@ const getRoutePath = (path: string): string => {
     dash: true,
     dot: true,
   });
-  const next = hasParam(rest) ? getRoutePath(rest) : rest;
+  const next = hasParam(rest) ? getRoutePath(rest) : esc(rest);
 
   return hasParam(path)
-    ? `${prev}\${${param}}${next}`
-    : `${prev}${param}${next}`;
+    ? `${esc(prev)}\${${param}}${next}`
+    : `${esc(prev)}${param}${next}`;
 };
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces the `jsesc` library to properly escape string values before embedding them into JavaScript template literals. It adds a `formatDefaultValue` function to handle various data types, ensuring that string defaults and array items are correctly escaped. Additionally, all keys in generated objects and segments of route paths are now explicitly JSON-stringified or `jsesc`-escaped to prevent injection.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 20 occurrences**

## GHSA-2w86-xfrc-g85r · orval

2026-09-03 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient sanitization of user-controlled input, specifically property names in schema definitions and parts of OpenAPI paths. These unsanitized strings were directly embedded into generated JavaScript code (template literals or object property keys), allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary JavaScript code by crafting malicious property names or path segments.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve Remote Code Execution (RCE) in the context where the generated code is executed, potentially leading to full system compromise or data exfiltration.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/zod/src/index.ts
+++ b/packages/zod/src/index.ts

         .map(([key, value]) => {

           if (isString(value)) {
-            return `${key}: ${JSON.stringify(value)} as const`;
+            return `${safeKey}: ${JSON.stringify(value)} as const`;
           }
 
           if (Array.isArray(value)) {
             const arrayItems = value.map((item) =>
               isString(item) ? `${JSON.stringify(item)} as const` : `${item}`,
             );
-            return `${key}: [${arrayItems.join(', ')}]`;
+            return `${safeKey}: [${arrayItems.join(', ')}]`;
           }
 
           if (
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `jsesc` for robust JavaScript string escaping. It ensures that all user-controlled strings, such as schema property names and OpenAPI path segments, are properly escaped before being embedded into generated JavaScript code, preventing injection attacks. Specifically, `JSON.stringify` is used for object keys and `jsesc` for string values and path segments.

## GHSA-6437-gxhq-pqv8 · orval

2026-09-03 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient sanitization of user-controlled input, specifically header parameter names and default values, when generating client-side code. These unsanitized values were directly embedded into JavaScript template literals as computed property keys or string literals, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary JavaScript code.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution during the import-time of the generated client code. This means that when a developer imports the generated client, the malicious code would execute, potentially leading to compromise of the developer's system or build environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/zod/src/index.ts
+++ b/packages/zod/src/index.ts
@@ -816,15 +834,16 @@ export const generateZodValidationSchemaDefinition = (
       // `.default()` rejects against its mutable parameter type (#3399).
       const entries = Object.entries(schema.default)
         .map(([key, value]) => {
+          const safeKey = JSON.stringify(key);
           if (isString(value)) {
-            return `${key}: ${JSON.stringify(value)} as const`;
+            return `${safeKey}: ${JSON.stringify(value)} as const`;
           }
 
           if (Array.isArray(value)) {
             const arrayItems = value.map((item) =>
               isString(item) ? `${JSON.stringify(item)} as const` : `${item}`,
             );
-            return `${key}: [${arrayItems.join(', ')}]`;
+            return `${safeKey}: [${arrayItems.join(', ')}]`;
           }
 
           if (
@@ -833,17 +852,13 @@ export const generateZodValidationSchemaDefinition = (
             isNumber(value) ||
             isBoolean(value)
           )
-            return `${key}: ${value}`;
+            return `${safeKey}: ${value}`;
         })
         .join(', ');
       defaultValue = entries.length === 0 ? `{}` : `{ ${entries} }`;
     } else {
       // OpenApiSchemaObject defines default as 'any'
-      const rawStringified = stringify(schema.default);
-      defaultValue =
-        rawStringified === undefined
-          ? 'null'
-          : rawStringified.replaceAll("'", '`');
+      defaultValue = formatDefaultValue(schema.default);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces the `jsesc` library to properly escape all user-controlled strings before they are embedded into generated JavaScript code, especially within template literals or as object keys. It also adds a `formatDefaultValue` function to handle various data types safely. This prevents injection by ensuring that input is treated as literal string data rather than executable code.

### Fix summary

The patch introduces the `jsesc` library to properly escape string values before embedding them into generated code. It modifies the `generateZodValidationSchemaDefinition` and `getRoutePath` functions to use `JSON.stringify` for object keys and `jsesc` for string defaults and route path segments, preventing injection into computed property keys and template literals.

## GHSA-8j6p-r8jg-mxqh · orval

2026-09-03 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from improper sanitization of user-controlled input when generating code. Specifically, default values for header parameters and parts of the OpenAPI path were directly embedded into JavaScript template literals without sufficient escaping, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary code.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution during the import-time of the generated code, leading to full compromise of the system where the generated code is executed.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/zod/src/index.ts
+++ b/packages/zod/src/index.ts
@@ -849,10 +867,7 @@ export const generateZodValidationSchemaDefinition = (
       defaultValue = entries.length === 0 ? `{}` : `{ ${entries} }`;
     } else {
       // OpenApiSchemaObject defines default as 'any'
-      const rawStringified = stringify(schema.default);
-      defaultValue =
-        rawStringified === undefined
-          ? 'null'
-          : rawStringified.replaceAll("'", '`');
+      defaultValue = formatDefaultValue(schema.default);
 
       // If the schema is an array with enum items, inject inplace to avoid issues with default values
       const isArrayWithEnumItems =
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces the `jsesc` library to properly escape user-controlled strings before embedding them into template literals. It adds a `formatDefaultValue` function for handling various default value types and ensures that all keys and string values in generated objects and paths are correctly JSON-stringified or JavaScript-escaped.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 20 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, PHP, Python, JavaScript
> → GHSA-xj4f-8jjg-vx4q
> → GHSA-x7m9-mwc2-g6w2
> → GHSA-gg2g-p7xc-qqmm

## GHSA-w727-8j6c-2rj4 · orval

2026-09-03 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient escaping of user-controlled input when generating code, specifically within template literals. Malicious input in schema default values or route paths could inject arbitrary JavaScript, leading to code execution during the import process.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution on the system where the generated code is imported, potentially leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/zod/src/index.ts
+++ b/packages/zod/src/index.ts
@@ -852,9 +870,7 @@ export const generateZodValidationSchemaDefinition = (
       defaultValue = entries.length === 0 ? `{}` : `{ ${entries} }`;
     } else {
       // OpenApiSchemaObject defines default as 'any'
-      const rawStringified = stringify(schema.default);
-      defaultValue =
-        rawStringified === undefined
-          ? 'null'
-          : rawStringified.replaceAll("'", '`');
+      defaultValue = formatDefaultValue(schema.default);
 
       // If the schema is an array with enum items, inject inplace to avoid issues with default values
       const isArrayWithEnumItems =
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces the `jsesc` library to properly escape string values before embedding them into template literals. It also adds a `formatDefaultValue` function to handle various data types safely and ensures all object keys and route path segments are correctly escaped using `JSON.stringify` or `jsesc`.
